Invite groups to calendar events

You can use Groups to quickly invite lots of people to an event at once. When you invite groups, you can have up to 100,000 guests.

Invite a group to an event

Important: To prevent spam, Google Calendar may limit the number of invitations you can send based on your account type. Learn more about Calendar usage guidelines.

Current members of a group can view an event on their calendar.

  • Go to Google Calendar.

  • Enter the email address for a group the same way you’d add any guest. Tips:

  • If you have “View members” access to a group and create a group event, each member receives an invitation email.

  • If you don’t have “View members” access, the group receives an invitation. For the event to display on a user’s calendar, each user has to accept the invite from the invitation email.

  • If you invite large groups of guests to an event, in the event, you’ll see an invitation in progress message until each guest is invited.

  • You can find RSVPs for groups within groups, but not for any additional nested groups. Learn how to add a group to another group.

View responses

You can view the RSVPs in the group if you:

  • Are the event organizer
  • Have edit rights to the event
  • Go to Google Calendar.
  • To view responses, next to the group , click the Arrow .
    • If you don’t find the group icon and arrow, you don’t have permission to view the group’s member list. The guest list matches the group membership for upcoming events. It won’t change after an event has passed. If your event has more than 200 guests, membership changes to the guest list take effect in 24 hours.

Group membership updates

When you invite a group to an event, the guest list updates to match any changes in the group. If new group members have email notifications turned on, they receive an email notification if the event is in the future.

Learn how to turn off email notifications for new events.

View & export RSVP list for groups larger than 200

Important: To follow these steps, you must have edit rights to an event of 200 people or more.

When you create an event for large groups, you can view and download guest’s replies, email address, and any added notes.

In large group events, you can’t:

  • Modify the events.
  • Invite others.
  • View the guest list.
  • Transfer the event to another owner.
  • Move the event to a different calendar.
  • On your computer, open the event on the original calendar from which the event was created.
  • Select Export guest responses to Sheets. Google Sheets will make a list from the most current RSVP list.
  • To update the information, repeat step 2 above. The export will create a new tab at the beginning of the original Sheet with the most up to date information. Note: You can only view RSVP status of members of groups that you have permissions to see.

Troubleshoot

I can’t reply Yes, Maybe, or No & the event wasn’t added to my calendar

If you can’t reply by clicking links in the invitation, this means the event organizer doesn’t have permission to view the group’s members.

To add the event to your calendar, you must instead click Add to calendar in the top section of the invitation. The event organizer won’t receive any RSVP.

I can’t view group members in an event, or their responses

This means you or the organizer don’t have permission to view group members, or that the group has more than 200 total guests. See above to learn how this is counted.

I can’t modify or invite more people to the event

Guests can’t modify the event or invite more people. Check with the event creator for access to edit the event.

Invite people to your Calendar event

You can add anyone with an email address to your event, even if they don’t have Google Calendar.

Add people to your event

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click an event Edit event .
  • On the right, under “Guests,” start typing the name of the person and choose someone from your contacts. You can also type an email address to invite people who aren’t in your contacts list.
    • To mark a guest as optional, next to the guest’s name, click Mark optional .
      • If the icon doesn’t show, hover over the guest’s name.
  • When you’re done editing your event, click Save. You can’t add people to events that are automatically created from Gmail as well as birthdays, holidays, and events on sports calendars.

Invite people who don’t use Google Calendar

You can invite people who don’t use Google Calendar to your event. Just follow the steps above and invite the person using their email address.

Your guests will receive an email invitation. They can let you know if they are going by clicking the Yes, No, or Maybe links in the email.

Tip: For the most reliable experience, use Google Calendar with your Google Account. If you use Google Calendar with a non-Google email address, you may encounter issues when you send invitations and receive RSVPs.

Give your guests updates on an event

Send event invitations and updates

When you update an event with guests on Google Calendar, you have the option to email them invitations.

To make guests aware of the event and updates, we recommend you send these emails. Guests won’t get an email if:

  • They opt out of email notifications for changes to their calendar. You can also choose not to send email notifications. Guests’ calendars are still updated, unless:

  • Google Calendar users have the setting “Only if the sender is known” and they haven’t interacted with you, don’t have your email in their contacts, and aren’t in the same domain.

  • They’ve chosen to not automatically add events to their calendar. Tip: Guests’ calendars always update after they click “Yes” or “Maybe” in the invitation email.

Non-Google Calendar users and Google Calendar users with a non-Gmail email provider get an email, which updates their calendar per the settings of their calendar service.

Use event notifications

You can set notifications to remind yourself of the event beforehand. Events you create use the default notification from your settings. You can add up to 5 notifications for each event. These notifications:

  • Aren’t shared with Google Calendar guests. Your Google Calendar guests receive reminders for your event based on their own settings.
  • Are shared with non-Google Calendar guests. Based on the calendar service your guests use, they get the notifications you set up or the notifications they select in their calendar. Learn more about setting event-level notifications.

Help your guests not miss the event

If you’re not in the same organization or guests haven’t interacted with you in the past, it’s possible your guest may need to RSVP through the email invitation before the event gets added to their calendar.

We recommend you remind them through your preferred communication channel to RSVP to the event in the email by clicking “Yes” or “Maybe”.

Email or chat with invited guests

Calendar only creates a group chat with guests invited to an event when there are no more than 200 guests (including the organizer). External guests aren’t added to group chats.

Important: The chat feature is only available if you use Google Calendar through a work or school account.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click an event.
  • Next to the number of guests, select an option:
    • Chat with guests : A new or existing chat opens. Only Chat-enabled guests in your work or school organization are included in the chat.
    • Email guests : A new email opens. All invited guests are included in the email. For more email format options:
  • On your computer, go to Gmail.
  • On the right side bar, click Calendar .
  • Click an event.
  • Next to the number of guests, select Email guests : A new email opens. All invited guests are included in the email.

Find a meeting time

If someone has shared their calendar with you, or is part of your work, school, or other organization, you can check if they’re free or busy when adding them to an event.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • On the left, click Search for people.
  • Start typing someone’s name and choose the person you want to meet with.
  • At the top left, click Create .
  • Click Find a time. If the guest you invited has shared their calendar with you, you’ll see when they can attend.
Guests in other time zones

If a guest is in another time zone and has shared their calendar with you, you’ll see when their meeting starts under their name at the top. Their meeting time is based on their current time zone. Learn more about sharing calendars.

Forward a calendar invitation email

Important: If you forward an invitation, the recipient might be able to see updated meeting details and could change your RSVP response at any time.

If you have permission to add guests to an event, you can forward the invite to new guests by email. When new guests respond to the invite, they’ll be added to the guest list. They’ll also be able to invite others.

If you don’t want guests to be able to invite others, edit your event and under “Guests can:” uncheck the box next to “invite others.”

Track responses to your event

When guests receive your invitation email, they can respond using the Yes, No, or Maybe links in the email. When a guest RSVPs to your event, you receive an email notification and your event updates.

To see who responded to your event, just click the event on your calendar. You’ll see who’s attending, RSVP status, and details.

Tip: In your settings, you can opt out of email notifications for RSVPs.

Troubleshoot problems

Guest limit for invitations

  • You can invite up to 200 people to an event.
  • To invite more people, use Google Groups. You can invite groups that have up to 100,000 people. Learn how to invite groups to calendar events. Invitation not received

If a guest didn’t receive an invitation to your event, you can ask them to:

  • Check their email inbox and RSVP to the email.
  • Check the spam folder in their email.
  • Check that they didn’t turn off email notifications for new events.
    • If the guest uses Google Calendar, ask them to check their email notifications on a computer. You can also remove the guest from the event and add them again.

Suggested times are wrong

Times are suggested based on a guest’s availability and working hours.

If someone’s working hours are set incorrectly, you won’t get the best suggested times.

Invite people to your Calendar event (Computer)

You can add anyone with an email address to your event, even if they don’t have Google Calendar.

Add people to your event

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click an event Edit event .
  • On the right, under “Guests,” start typing the name of the person and choose someone from your contacts. You can also type an email address to invite people who aren’t in your contacts list.
    • To mark a guest as optional, next to the guest’s name, click Mark optional .
      • If the icon doesn’t show, hover over the guest’s name.
  • When you’re done editing your event, click Save. You can’t add people to events that are automatically created from Gmail as well as birthdays, holidays, and events on sports calendars.

Invite people who don’t use Google Calendar

You can invite people who don’t use Google Calendar to your event. Just follow the steps above and invite the person using their email address.

Your guests will receive an email invitation. They can let you know if they are going by clicking the Yes, No, or Maybe links in the email.

Tip: For the most reliable experience, use Google Calendar with your Google Account. If you use Google Calendar with a non-Google email address, you may encounter issues when you send invitations and receive RSVPs.

Give your guests updates on an event

Send event invitations and updates

When you update an event with guests on Google Calendar, you have the option to email them invitations.

To make guests aware of the event and updates, we recommend you send these emails. Guests won’t get an email if:

  • They opt out of email notifications for changes to their calendar. You can also choose not to send email notifications. Guests’ calendars are still updated, unless:

  • Google Calendar users have the setting “Only if the sender is known” and they haven’t interacted with you, don’t have your email in their contacts, and aren’t in the same domain.

  • They’ve chosen to not automatically add events to their calendar. Tip: Guests’ calendars always update after they click “Yes” or “Maybe” in the invitation email.

Non-Google Calendar users and Google Calendar users with a non-Gmail email provider get an email, which updates their calendar per the settings of their calendar service.

Use event notifications

Invite people to your Calendar event (iPhone and iPad)

You can add anyone with an email address to your event, even if they don’t have Google Calendar.

Add people to your event

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .
  • Edit or create an event you want to add people to.
  • Tap Edit .
  • Tap Add guests.
  • Enter the name or email address of the person you want to invite.
    • To mark a guest as optional, next to the guest’s name, tap Mark optional .
  • Tap Done.
    • To find when your guests are available, swipe down or tap View schedules​.​
  • Tap Save. When you save your event, we’ll send an email invitation to your guests.

You can’t add people to events that are automatically created from Gmail, as well as birthdays, holidays, and events on sports calendars.

Options for inviting guests

Invite people who don’t use Google Calendar

You can invite people who don’t use Google Calendar to your event. Follow the steps above and invite the person using their email address.

Invite people using Google Groups

You can invite a Google Group to your event instead of inviting people one by one. Follow the steps above and add the email address for the group. Learn more about inviting groups to your event.

See who is coming to your event

When guests receive your invitation email, they can respond using the Yes, No, or Maybe links in the email.

To see who responded to your event:

  • Open the Google Calendar app .
  • Open the event.
  • In the “Guests” section, you’ll see who is attending.
Find a meeting time

​If you got your Google Account through your work, school, or group, you may find guests’ schedules. Find a meeting time that works for everyone.

  • Open the Google Calendar app .
  • Create or edit an event.
  • Add your guests.
  • Tap Find a time.
  • Choose a suggested time. Compare schedules: Tap More and choose a time.

Filter meeting times: On the “Find time” page, tap Change. You can change the duration of the meeting and the timeframe when it should happen.

Editing events: You can only edit the event if you created the event yourself, or if the event organizer gave guests permission to change the event.

Forward a calendar invitation email

Important: If you forward an invitation, the recipient might be able to see updated meeting details and could change your RSVP response at any time.

If you have permission to add guests to an event, you can forward the invite to new guests by email. When new guests respond to the invite, they’ll be added to the guest list. They’ll also be able to invite others.

If you don’t want guests to be able to invite others, edit your event, tap the guests, and turn off “Guests can add others.”

Email or chat with invited guests

Calendar only creates a group chat with guests invited to an event when there are no more than 200 guests (including the organizer). External guests aren’t added to group chats.

    • Email guests : A new email opens. All invited guests are included in the email.

Guest limit for invitations

Important: Guest lists are hidden for events with more than 50 guests.

  • You can invite up to 200 people to an event.

Manage your events from Gmail (Computer)

If you use Gmail, emails about your events, like flights or reservations, are added to your calendar. When you open the event in Google Calendar, you can find details about the event. This includes the address, reference number, and link to the source email.

If you don’t want an event from Gmail on your calendar, you can delete it. You can also update your settings so events from Gmail aren’t added to your calendar.

Learn how events from Gmail work

  • You get events from Gmail in Google Calendar for:
    • Flights, trains, and bus reservations.
    • Hotel and restaurant reservations.
    • Events for which you get registration confirmations or tickets. This includes movies, shows, and medical events.
  • Events from Gmail stay up-to-date on your calendar. For example, if you change a reservation, the calendar event also changes. Your changes show up only on your calendar.
  • Event notifications are based on your default notification settings in Calendar.
  • By default, only you can find events from Gmail, even if you share your calendar with others. You can change the visibility of events from Gmail. Tip: If you sync Calendar with another program, you still get events from Gmail. However, some details don’t show up.

Add all events from Gmail

Important:

  • By default, events from Gmail are off if you’re in:
    • The European Economic Area
    • Japan
    • Switzerland
    • The UK
  • For all other places, events from Gmail are on. If you don’t get events from Gmail, check your Google Workspace smart features settings.
Step 1: Turn on Google Workspace smart features
  • On your computer, go to Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • Under “General,” click Google Workspace smart features Manage Workspace smart feature settings.
  • Turn on Smart features in Google Workspace.
  • Click Save. Tip: If you don’t want Gmail to add events to your calendar, turn off Smart features in Google Workspace.
Step 2: Turn on events from Gmail
  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • Under “General,” click Google Workspace smart features.
  • Check the box next to “Show events from Gmail.” Turn on events from Gmail

Delete an event from Gmail

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click the event from Gmail Delete event .

Hide all events from Gmail

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, click Google Workspace smart features.
  • Uncheck the box next to “Show events from Gmail.”
  • In the pop-up, click OK.

Learn about the limits for events from Gmail

  • Events don’t show up on your calendar if:
    • The email goes to a mail list.
    • You’re cc’d on the email.
    • The email redirects from another email account.
    • The email isn’t a confirmation for a flight, hotel, restaurant, or ticketed event.
  • Events from Gmail don’t work for:
    • Government accounts.
    • Google Accounts with data location restrictions.
    • Google Accounts that don’t have Gmail.

Report problems with events from Gmail

When Gmail finds dates and times in an email, an event may be automatically added to your calendar. If that happens and there’s a problem, like if the calendar event’s date or time is wrong, let us know.

You can send feedback about events from Gmail if:

  • The supported event type doesn’t show up on your calendar.
  • The event has wrong or missing details.
  • The event isn’t for you.
    • To find the source email of the event, open the event.
      • In the Calendar app: Select View source email.
      • In Calendar on your browser: Select View confirmation.
Step 1: Copy the email
  • On your computer, go to mail.google.com.
  • Find the email that contains the event.
  • At the top right of the email, click More Show original.
  • Click Copy to clipboard.
Step 2: Report the problem
  • On your computer, go to calendar.google.com.
  • At the top right, click Support Send feedback.
  • In the text field, describe the problem.
  • Enter the email by pasting your clipboard.
    • Windows: Ctrl + v.
    • Mac: Command + v.
  • If you want to, highlight or hide info in the screenshot.
  • Click Send.

Related resources

  • Manage the visibility of events from Gmail
  • Report problems with events from Gmail
  • Smart features & controls in Google products

Use focus time in Google Calendar

To reduce distractions while you work, you can schedule a focus time event in Google Calendar.

When you schedule a focus time event, you can:

  • Mute chat notifications.
  • Automatically decline meetings.

Learn who can use focus time

To schedule a focus time event, you need to have an eligible work or school account:

  • Google Workspace for Business Standard and Plus
  • Google Workspace for Enterprise Standard and Plus
  • Google Workspace for Education
  • Google Workspace for Nonprofits

Schedule focus time

Important: You can only create focus time events from Day and Week views. The events must have a specific start and end time.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.

  • Click the time you want to schedule focus time.

  • At the top of the event, click Focus time.

  • Select when you want your event to begin and end.

  • Set your focus time preferences:

    • To receive notifications for Chat messages, next to “Do not disturb,” deselect the box.
    • To automatically decline meetings during focus time, next to “Automatically decline meetings,” check the box.

  • Click Save. Tips:

  • When you deselect Do not disturb, it becomes the default setting for new focus time events.

  • By default, focus time declines new and existing meetings. However, you can update your focus time settings to “Only decline new meetings.”

Edit or delete focus time

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click the focus time event.
    • Focus time events are marked with headphones .
  • Select an option:
    • To edit the event:
      • Click Edit .
      • Make your edits.
      • Click Save.
    • To delete the event, click Delete event .

Change how meetings are declined during focus time

  • Click the focus time event.
  • Click Edit .
  • Under “Automatically decline meetings,” select:
    • Only new meeting invitations
    • New and existing meetings
  • Click Save.

Repeat a focus time event

  • Click the focus time event.
  • Click Edit .
  • Next to “Does not repeat,” click the Down arrow .
  • Select how often you want to repeat your focus time event.
  • Click Save.

Change privacy settings on a focus time event

  • Click the focus time event.
  • Click Edit .
  • Under the calendar name, click Default visibility.
  • Next to “Default visibility,” click the Down arrow .
  • Select Public or Private.

Change the color of a focus time event

  • Click the focus time event.
  • Click Edit .
  • Click Color .
  • Select a color.
  • Click Save. Tip: Your color selection is saved for future focus time events.

Learn about focus time & Chat notifications

  • You can only choose to mute Chat notifications if:
    • Your organization turns on Chat.
    • The focus time block is less than 24 hours.
  • The first time you use focus time, “Do not disturb” for Chat notifications is turned on by default.
  • When you create or edit focus time, you can turn off Chat notifications.

Related resources

  • Set your working hours & location
  • Modify Google Calendar notifications

Use focus time in Google Calendar (Computer)

To reduce distractions while you work, you can schedule a focus time event in Google Calendar.

When you schedule a focus time event, you can:

  • Mute chat notifications.
  • Automatically decline meetings.

Learn who can use focus time

To schedule a focus time event, you need to have an eligible work or school account:

  • Google Workspace for Business Standard and Plus
  • Google Workspace for Enterprise Standard and Plus
  • Google Workspace for Education
  • Google Workspace for Nonprofits

Schedule focus time

Important: You can only create focus time events from Day and Week views. The events must have a specific start and end time.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.

  • Click the time you want to schedule focus time.

  • At the top of the event, click Focus time.

  • Select when you want your event to begin and end.

  • Set your focus time preferences:

    • To receive notifications for Chat messages, next to “Do not disturb,” deselect the box.
    • To automatically decline meetings during focus time, next to “Automatically decline meetings,” check the box.

  • Click Save. Tips:

  • When you deselect Do not disturb, it becomes the default setting for new focus time events.

  • By default, focus time declines new and existing meetings. However, you can update your focus time settings to “Only decline new meetings.”

Edit or delete focus time

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click the focus time event.
    • Focus time events are marked with headphones .
  • Select an option:
    • To edit the event:
      • Click Edit .
      • Make your edits.
      • Click Save.
    • To delete the event, click Delete event .

Change how meetings are declined during focus time

  • Click the focus time event.
  • Click Edit .
  • Under “Automatically decline meetings,” select:
    • Only new meeting invitations
    • New and existing meetings
  • Click Save.

Repeat a focus time event

  • Click the focus time event.
  • Click Edit .
  • Next to “Does not repeat,” click the Down arrow .
  • Select how often you want to repeat your focus time event.
  • Click Save.

Change privacy settings on a focus time event

  • Click the focus time event.
  • Click Edit .
  • Under the calendar name, click Default visibility.
  • Next to “Default visibility,” click the Down arrow .
  • Select Public or Private.

Change the color of a focus time event

  • Click the focus time event.
  • Click Edit .
  • Click Color .
  • Select a color.
  • Click Save. Tip: Your color selection is saved for future focus time events.

Learn about focus time & Chat notifications

  • You can only choose to mute Chat notifications if:
    • Your organization turns on Chat.
    • The focus time block is less than 24 hours.
  • The first time you use focus time, “Do not disturb” for Chat notifications is turned on by default.
  • When you create or edit focus time, you can turn off Chat notifications.

Related resources

  • Set your working hours & location
  • Modify Google Calendar notifications

Use focus time in Google Calendar (iPhone and iPad)

To reduce distractions while you work, you can schedule a focus time event in Google Calendar.

When you schedule a focus time event, you can:

  • Mute chat notifications.
  • Automatically decline meetings.

Learn who can use focus time

To schedule a focus time event, you need to have an eligible work or school account:

  • Google Workspace for Business Standard and Plus
  • Google Workspace for Enterprise Standard and Plus
  • Google Workspace for Education
  • Google Workspace for Nonprofits

Schedule focus time

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .

  • At the bottom right, tap CreateFocus time.

  • Select when you want your event to begin and end.

  • Set your focus time preferences:

    • To mute Google Chat notifications: Turn on Do not disturb.
    • To automatically decline meetings:
      • Turn on Automatically decline meetings
      • Decline New and existing meetings or Only new meeting invitations.

  • Optional: For your focus time event, you can also:

    • Set a custom color .
    • Add a description.
    • Change the event visibility settings .

  • At the top right, tap Save. Tips:

  • When you turn off Do not disturb, it becomes the default setting for new focus time events.

  • By default, focus time declines new and existing meetings. However, you can update your focus time settings to Only new meeting invitations.

Edit or delete focus time

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .
  • Tap a focus time event.
    • Tip: Focus time events are marked with headphones .
  • Select an option:
    • To edit the event:
      • At the top right, tap Edit .
      • Make your edits.
      • Tap Save.
    • To delete the event: At the top right, tap More options Delete Delete.

Learn about focus time & Chat notifications

  • You can only choose to mute Chat notifications if:
    • Your organization turns on Chat.
    • The focus time block is less than 24 hours.
  • The first time you use focus time, “Do not disturb” for Chat notifications is turned on by default.
  • When you create or edit focus time, you can turn off Chat notifications.

Related resources

  • Set your working hours & location
  • Modify Google Calendar notifications

Customize your calendar settings

Add another account to Google Calendar

In Google Calendar, you can add more than one account.

Add an account

  • On your Android phone or tablet, open the Google Calendar app .
  • At the top right, tap your Profile photo Add another account.
  • To finish the sign-in process, follow the instructions on the screen.

Turn off auto-sync for an account

Important:

  • To save data and battery life, you can limit your sync to happen only while you use the app. For example, when you open your calendar or create and change events.
  • If you close the Calendar app while a sync’s in progress, it might take some time before the sync stops.
  • On your Android phone or tablet, open the Google Calendar app .
  • At the top right, tap your Profile photo Manage accounts on this device.
  • Select an account.
  • Tap Account sync.
  • Turn off Calendar.

Remove an account

  • On your Android phone or tablet, open the Google Calendar app .
  • At the top right, tap your Profile photo Manage accounts on this device.
  • Select an account.
  • Tap Remove account.

Related resources

  • Switch between accounts in the Google Calendar app
  • Create a new calendar

Add another account to Google Calendar (iPhone and iPad)

In Google Calendar, you can add more than one account.

Add an account

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .
  • At the top right, tap your Profile photo Add another account.
  • To finish the sign-in process, follow the instructions on the screen.

Turn off auto-sync for an account

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .
  • At the top right, tap your Profile photo Manage accounts on this device.
  • Turn off your account.

Remove an account

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .
  • At the top right, tap your Profile photo.
  • Tap Manage accounts on this device Remove from this device Remove.

Related resources

  • Switch between accounts in the Google Calendar app
  • Create a new calendar

Add Google Calendar to your home screen

To check your upcoming events and meetings from the home screen without opening the Google Calendar app, add a widget.

Add the Calendar widget to your home screen

Important: Some of these steps may differ based on your Android phone or tablet.

  • On your Android phone or tablet, touch and hold the home screen.
  • Tap Widgets Calendar.
  • Touch and hold one of these widgets:
    • Calendar schedule: View your tasks and upcoming events.
    • Calendar month view: Show your schedule one month at a time.
  • Drag the widget to an empty space on the home screen, then release it.

Resize the Calendar widget

Important: In the Android widget, you can’t resize the button for Create .

  • On your Android phone or tablet, touch and hold the widget on your home screen, then release it.
    • An outline with dots appears on the sides of the widget.
  • To resize the widget, drag the dots.
  • When you’re done, tap outside the widget.

Move the Calendar widget

  • On your Android phone or tablet, touch and hold the widget.
  • Drag the widget to an empty space on the home screen, then release it.

Remove the Calendar widget

  • On your Android phone or tablet, touch and hold the widget.
  • Drag the widget up to “Remove,” then release it.

Related resources

  • Create an event
  • Change Google Calendar settings

Add Google Calendar to your home screen (iPhone and iPad)

To check your upcoming events and meetings from the home screen without opening the Google Calendar app, add a widget.

Add the Calendar widget to your home screen

Important:

  • You can add widgets on an iPhone or iPad running iOS 14 and up.
  • To add the widget, download the Calendar app.
  • If you recently installed the Calendar app, you may need to open the app before the widget appears in the list of widgets.
  • On your iPhone or iPad, touch and hold the home screen.
  • Review the steps for how to add widgets from Apple’s website:
    • How to add and edit widgets on your iPhone
    • Add, edit, and remove widgets on iPad
  • In the widget list, look for Google Calendar.
  • Add your preferred widget.

Related resources

  • Create an event
  • Change Google Calendar settings

Change event & task visibility on your calendar

In Google Calendar, you can change the visibility of individual events and tasks to control what others can find.

There are 3 visibility settings:

Visibility setting

Description

Example

Default visibility

The “Default visibility” setting follows the main sharing settings for your calendar.

If you share your calendar with colleagues and give them the “See event details” permission or higher, they can find details about your event.

However, if you only share your calendar with “See free/busy” permission, they will only see you as busy.

To change the main sharing settings for your calendar, learn how to choose what others can do with your calendar.

Regular meetings: Use this setting for your standard team syncs, working blocks, or project check-ins that follow your normal calendar sharing rules.

Public

Anyone who has access to view your calendar can find all the details of this specific event or task, even if your overall calendar is restricted to only show free or busy.

Social or out-of-office events: Use this for a team lunch or an out-of-office block where you want everyone to know where you are or how to reach you in an emergency.

Private

This setting hides all details from everyone except you and people you’ve given “Make changes to events and see event details” and higher access permissions.

Others looking at your calendar will see a block labeled “Busy.”

Appointments or confidential meetings: Use this setting for personal appointments, performance reviews, or sensitive project meetings where the topic should remain confidential.

Change event visibility

  • On your computer, go to Google Calendar.
  • In your calendar, open an event.
  • Click Edit event Default visibility.
  • Select a visibility setting:
    • Default visibility
    • Public
    • Private
  • Click Save.

Change task visibility

Important: If your task has a start and end time, you can change its visibility. Tasks without a start or end time are always private and are never visible to others.

  • On your computer, go to Google Calendar.
  • In your calendar, open a task.
  • Click Edit task Advanced settings .
  • Select a visibility setting:
    • Default visibility
    • Public
    • Private
  • Click Save.

Compare visibility settings

Use the table below to understand how visibility options interact with your primary calendar. If there’s a checkmark , it means that all event details are shown to anyone with access to your calendar.

Visibility setting

Permission

See free/busy

See event details

Make changes (see private events as free/busy)

Make changes and see event details

Make changes and manage sharing

Public

Default visibility

Free / busy only

Private

Free / busy only

Free / busy only

Free / busy only

Learn more about visibility settings

Understand event visibility

  • If you don’t share your calendar or make it public, your events:

    • Aren’t shared with others
    • Are only visible to you

  • If you set your calendar to only show events as Free or Busy for everyone, event details can be visible to others to whom you assigned different sharing permissions. To limit the visibility of individual events, set the event to Private.

  • If you grant someone the “Make changes (see private events as free/busy)” permission, they see your private entries differently based on their status. Any private event or task marked as Busy appears as a busy block. Those marked as Free are hidden from their view of your calendar grid.

  • When you invite someone to an event, they can control how your event shows on their calendar.

    • If your event is set to Default visibility and your invitee has shared their calendar with others, those people can find the event details on the invitee’s calendar.
    • If your event is set to Default visibility and you book a meeting room or other resource, people with access to the resource’s calendar can also find the event details.

  • Even if you set your event as private, some details might still be visible to others when you invite guests or book rooms. These details may include:

    • Event start time
    • Event end time
    • Event creator

  • Recurring events can only have one visibility setting. All events in a series must be entirely default, private, or public. Any attempt to change the visibility for a single instance or a subset of events in a series applies to the entire series. Understand task visibility

  • By default, even if you share your calendar, your tasks with a start and end time stay private unless you give others the “Make changes and see event details” permission or higher to your calendar, or you set a different visibility for a specific task.

  • If you set the task with a start and end time to Busy, your task becomes visible to others. However, the task details are hidden unless you give others the “Make changes and see event details” permission or higher to your calendar, or you set a different visibility for this specific task.

  • When you change calendar sharing settings, these apply to future tasks that you create.

Related resources

  • Manage your events from Gmail
  • Share your calendar

  • At the top right, tap Edit .
  • In the event details, tap Visibility settings .
  • Select a visibility setting:
    • Default visibility
    • Private
    • Public
  • Tap Save.

Change event color set & density in Google Calendar

In Google Calendar, you can customize the appearance of your events. You can change the color of the event text and adjust the spacing between events.

Important:

  • These settings are only available when you use Google Calendar on your computer.
  • To change the color set, set your background to “Light.” Learn how to change the background.
  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings Appearance.
  • Select a color set:
    • Modern (with white text)
    • Classic (with black text)
  • Select a density setting:
    • Responsive: Your calendar dynamically scales and enlarges events to fill larger monitors, which makes events easier to read.
    • Comfortable: Your calendar keeps the standard layout with traditional spacing, regardless of your screen size.
    • Compact: Your calendar shows a denser view with tighter spacing, which allows you to see more events at once.

Related resources

  • Use Dark theme in Calendar
  • Change your Calendar settings

Change Google Calendar notifications

To stay informed about your events, you can change or set up your notification settings. You get these notifications:

  • On your phone
  • In your web browser
  • By email You can change your notification settings for just one event or for many events.

Learn how notifications work on your account

  • Your notification settings are personal and apply only to your account, whether you use a personal, work, or school account.
  • No one else can change your notification settings, including people you share your calendar with or people can make changes to events on your calendar.
  • Your specific settings don’t affect the notifications of others who have access to the calendar.
  • Your calendar-level settings determine your notifications for new events, unless you customize them for a specific event.

Learn how to turn notifications on or off

You can get event notifications:

  • By email: You get the updates in your email.
  • As desktop notifications: When your calendar is open, these pop up outside your web browser.
  • As alerts: They show up within your Google Calendar window. Tip: If you don’t use Calendar, you get emails when someone creates, updates, or deletes an event you’re invited to.
Turn notifications on or off for all events

Important:

  • To change the settings, you need to open Calendar in a web browser, like Chrome or Safari.

  • If you’re asked to let “calendar.google.com” show notifications, you need to allow it. To change how notifications work for all your events:

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.

  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.

    • On the left, under “General,” click Notification settings.
    • Under “Notification settings,” you can:
      • Turn notifications on or off: Click the Notifications drop-down and select how you want to receive notifications.
      • Adjust the timing of snoozed notifications: Turn on “Desktop notifications,” then click Show snoozed notifications and customize your timing.
      • Only get notifications if you respond “Yes” or “Maybe” to an event: Click the box next to “Notify me only if I have responded Yes or Maybe.” Tip: You can get snooze notifications only on Chrome browser. If you have many notification times for the same event, the Snooze option shows only on the last one.
Turn notifications on or off for one event
  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click the event Edit event .
  • Next to Notifications :
    • To edit your notifications: Choose if you want to receive a notification or an email. You can change how often you want to get alerts in advance.
    • To add another type of notification: Click Add notification.
    • To remove a notification: Click Remove .
  • At the top, click Save.
Turn notifications on or off for specific calendars

To change how notifications work for specific calendars you own:

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, under “Settings for my calendars,” click the calendar you want to change Calendar settings.
  • Under “Event notifications” and “All-day event notifications:”
    • To edit your notifications: Choose if you want to receive a notification or an email. You can change how often you want to get alerts in advance.
    • To add another type of notification: Click Add notification.
    • To remove a notification: Click Remove notification .
Turn email notifications on or off for specific categories

To choose whether you receive email alerts for specific event changes:

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, under “Settings for my calendars,” click the name of the calendar.
  • Click Other notifications.
  • Next to each category, such as “Changed events” or “Event responses,” click the dropdown menu.
  • Select Email or None. Tip: If you manage multiple calendars, or receive a high volume of invitation emails, you can create custom Gmail filters to organize, label, or archive specific types of calendar updates. For example, you can search for RSVP declines with notes and archive routine accepted invitations. Learn how to filter and organize Calendar notifications in Gmail.

Fix issues when Calendar notifications don’t work

If you don’t get event notifications, update your browser settings to allow Google Calendar to send notifications. You should also make sure that:

  • Notifications for your calendar are on. Follow the steps above to check.
  • Your browser can show notifications. If not, turn on desktop notifications.
  • Google Calendar is still open in your web browser.
Google Chrome
  • On your computer, open Google Chrome .
  • At the top right, click More Settings.
  • Click Privacy and security Site settings Notifications.
  • Next to “Allowed to send notifications,” click Add.
  • In the “Site” field, enter “calendar.google.com”.
  • Click Add.
Other browsers

Important: For the exact instructions, check the support site of your browser:

  • Firefox
  • Safari
  • Edge
  • On your computer, open your web browser.
  • Go to your browser’s notification preferences.
  • Add permissions for calendar.google.com. Tip: If you have a work or school account, your admin can make changes to events on your calendar. When they make changes, you don’t get a notification.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click the event Edit event .
  • Next to Notifications :
    • To edit your notifications: Choose if you want to receive a notification or an email. You can change how often you want to get alerts in advance.
    • To add another type of notification: Click Add notification.
    • To remove a notification: Click Remove .
  • At the top, click Save.
Turn notifications on or off for specific calendars

To change how notifications work for specific calendars you own:

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, under “Settings for my calendars,” click the calendar you want to change Calendar settings.
  • Under “Event notifications” and “All-day event notifications:”
    • To edit your notifications: Choose if you want to receive a notification or an email. You can change how often you want to get alerts in advance.
    • To add another type of notification: Click Add notification.
    • To remove a notification: Click Remove notification .
Turn email notifications on or off for specific categories

To choose whether you receive email alerts for specific event changes:

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, under “Settings for my calendars,” click the name of the calendar.
  • Click Other notifications.
  • Next to each category, such as “Changed events” or “Event responses,” click the dropdown menu.
  • Select Email or None. Tip: If you manage multiple calendars, or receive a high volume of invitation emails, you can create custom Gmail filters to organize, label, or archive specific types of calendar updates. For example, you can search for RSVP declines with notes and archive routine accepted invitations. Learn how to filter and organize Calendar notifications in Gmail.

Fix issues when Calendar notifications don’t work

If you don’t get event notifications, update your browser settings to allow Google Calendar to send notifications. You should also make sure that:

  • Notifications for your calendar are on. Follow the steps above to check.
  • Your browser can show notifications. If not, turn on desktop notifications.
  • Google Calendar is still open in your web browser.
Google Chrome
  • On your computer, open Google Chrome .
  • At the top right, click More Settings.
  • Click Privacy and security Site settings Notifications.
  • Next to “Allowed to send notifications,” click Add.
  • In the “Site” field, enter “calendar.google.com”.
  • Click Add.
Other browsers

Important: For the exact instructions, check the support site of your browser:

  • Firefox
  • Safari
  • Edge
  • On your computer, open your web browser.
  • Go to your browser’s notification preferences.
  • Add permissions for calendar.google.com. Tip: If you have a work or school account, your admin can make changes to events on your calendar. When they make changes, you don’t get a notification.

Change Google Calendar notifications (iPhone and iPad)

To stay informed about your events, you can change or set up your notification settings. You get these notifications:

  • On your phone
  • In your web browser
  • By email You can change your notification settings for just one event or for many events.

Learn how notifications work on your account

  • Your notification settings are personal and apply only to your account, whether you use a personal, work, or school account.
  • No one else can change your notification settings, including people you share your calendar with or people can make changes to events on your calendar.
  • Your specific settings don’t affect the notifications of others who have access to the calendar.
  • Your calendar-level settings determine your notifications for new events, unless you customize them for a specific event.

Learn how to change your notification settings

Important: Any changes you make on your phone also sync with your computer notifications. For example, on your phone, if you set a “1 hour before” notification, you also get a pop-up notification on your computer one hour before the event.

You can choose to:

  • Change your default notifications for events that happen once and events that last all day.
  • Get notifications on your device, by email, or both. Change your notification settings
For a specific calendar

Important: Any changes you make to the notifications are only for you. Event guests get notifications based on their own settings.

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .
  • At the top left, tap Menu .
  • At the bottom, tap Settings .
  • Select one of your calendars.
  • Select Add another notification.
For just one event
  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .
  • Select an event.
  • Tap Edit .
  • To change or remove, in the “Notifications” section, tap a notification.
    • Or, to add a new one, tap Add another notification.
  • At the top right, tap Save.
Turn off notifications

If you want to turn off all the notifications for Calendar, change your calendar’s main settings. You can change the settings from your phone or computer as notifications sync across your devices.

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Calendar app .
  • At the top left, tap Menu .
  • At the bottom, tap Settings .
  • Select one of your calendars.
  • Swipe left on a notification.
  • Tap Delete.
    • Do the same for each notification. If you have more than one account or calendar, do these steps for each one.

Turn off notifications for another calendar app

If you get the same notifications from another calendar app, you can turn off notifications for the other app. These steps might differ for your device.

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Settings app.
  • Turn off notifications for the other calendar app.

Control Google Calendar data sharing with other apps

On your Android phone or tablet, you can manage how your Google Calendar data is shared with other apps. The “Share Google Calendar data with other apps” setting lets you manage access to your Calendar information, which gives you more privacy and control over your data.

Manage your Calendar data sharing settings

Important: If you share your Calendar information with other apps, you might get a pop-up notification in Google Calendar to manage data sharing in your Calendar settings. If you close the notification, you can follow the instructions in this article to adjust your data sharing preferences for Calendar.

  • On your Android phone or tablet, open the Calendar app .
  • Tap Menu Settings General.
  • Turn Share Google Calendar data with other apps on or off.

Learn about sharing Calendar data

  • The “Share Google Calendar data with other apps” setting controls whether your Calendar data is accessible via Calendar Provider to apps you granted Calendar permission to.
  • The setting protects your data from being accidentally accessed or corrupted by other apps you might have granted access to.
  • The following features and apps aren’t affected when you no longer share your Calendar data with other apps:
    • Google apps and experiences you’ve signed into with your Google Account.
    • Third-party apps you granted Google Calendar access to in other ways.
  • The following features and apps might be affected when you no longer share your Calendar data with other apps:
    • Third-party calendar apps: Other apps might not show or notify you about your Calendar events.
    • Third-party calendar widgets: Some of these widgets might not show Calendar events.
    • Other apps that aren’t primarily calendar-based: For example, apps for video conferencing or shift planning might not show Calendar data.
    • Certain Android system features: For example, a feature on Android that automatically turns on “Do not disturb” mode during a meeting is affected.

Related resources

  • Change Google Calendar settings
  • Sync Calendar with a phone or tablet

Filter & organize Calendar notifications in Gmail

To keep your inbox organized, you can use Gmail filters. This helps you manage calendars for other people or a team.

When Google Calendar sends an email, it contains a hidden email header called X-Google-Calendar-Notification, which Gmail can read. For example, this includes when a:

  • Meeting time changes.
  • Room is removed.
  • Guest adds a note to their reply.

Step 1: Set up notifications in Calendar

Before you create filters in Gmail, turn on the notifications you want in Google Calendar:

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, under “Settings for my calendars,” click the name of the calendar.
  • Click Other notifications.
  • Next to each category, such as “Changed events” or “Event responses,” click the dropdown menu.
  • Select Email or None.

Step 2: Create a filter in Gmail

After you set up your notification settings, you can use custom filters in Gmail to search for specific event notifications.

  • On your computer, open Gmail.
  • In the search box at the top, enter a query using a value from the table below.
    • For example, “header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:rsvpDeclined.”
  • On the right side of the search box, click Show search options .
  • At the bottom of the window, click Create filter.
  • Choose what you want the filter to do, such as archive, label, or delete the email.
  • Click Create filter.
Filter tokens for Calendar notifications

To filter a specific action, combine header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification: with one of the search tokens below. For example, to filter new event invitations, use header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:eventCreated.

Category

Notification action

Search token

New & cancelled events

New event invitation sent

eventCreated

New & cancelled events

Event cancelled

eventCancelled

Event updates

Time or recurrence rules changed

timeOrRecurrenceUpdated

Event updates

Meeting room added

roomAdded

Event updates

Meeting room removed

roomRemoved

Event updates

Location changed

locationUpdated

Event updates

Event title changed

titleUpdated

Event updates

Description changed

descriptionUpdated

Event updates

Document or attachment added

attachmentAdded

Event updates

Document or attachment removed

attachmentRemoved

Event updates

Video conferencing link updated

conferenceUpdated

Guest RSVPs

Guest accepted invitation

rsvpAccepted

Guest RSVPs

Guest declined invitation

rsvpDeclined

Guest RSVPs

Guest responded “Maybe”

rsvpTentative

Guest RSVPs

Guest included a note with RSVP

rsvpWithNote

Guest RSVPs

Guest proposed a new meeting time

rsvpProposeNewTime

Room RSVPs

Meeting room accepted booking

roomAccepted

Room RSVPs

Meeting room declined booking

roomDeclined

Here are a few ways you can combine these operators to organize your inbox:

  • To keep only RSVP declines with a note, and archive all other RSVPs:
    • To label important notes:
      • Create a filter with the query:
      • `header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:rsvpDeclined
      • header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:rsvpWithNote`.
      • Set the action to: Apply label “Urgent RSVPs.”
    • To archive routine RSVPs:
      • Create a filter with the query:
      • `header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:rsvpAccepted OR
      • header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:rsvpTentative OR
      • (header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:rsvpDeclined
      • -header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:rsvpWithNote).`
      • Set the action to: “Skip the Inbox (Archive it).”
  • To find a declined meeting room booking:
    • Create a filter with the query:
    • header:X-Google-Calendar-Notification:roomDeclined.
    • Set the action to: Apply label “Room Conflicts” or “Mark as important.”

  • Create rules to filter your emails

Learn about smart features & controls for Google Workspace & other Google products

To give you more granular control over your data, we’ve updated our smart feature settings for Google Workspace. This update turns the existing control into two distinct settings that govern different product experiences. These updates also give you more choice and control over your data, and do not alter our underlying data handling practices or our robust privacy commitments.

In Calendar, Chat, Drive, Gmail, Meet, and Voice, you can manage smart feature settings. Depending on your smart feature settings, you can do things like:

  • Use Gemini in Google Workspace to reference Drive files from Gmail.
  • Automatically add events such as flights from Gmail to Calendar.
  • Compile loyalty cards and tickets from your inbox into Google Wallet.
  • Track an upcoming order using summary cards in Gmail.
  • Record, transcribe, and summarize notes in Meet and Voice.

Smart features & controls for Google Workspace & other Google products

In this article

  • Learn about smart feature settings & experiences
  • Learn how smart features use your Google Workspace content & activity
  • Change smart feature settings
  • Learn how Google handles data in connection with smart feature settings

Learn about smart feature settings & experiences

Each Workspace smart feature setting works independently from one another and provides different experiences. For example, you can turn on smart features for Google Workspace, and turn off smart features for other Google products. To understand how each smart feature setting works, use the table below.

  • You can only turn on this setting from a web browser.

Smart features in Gmail, Chat & Meet

When you turn on smart features in Gmail, Chat, and Meet, you agree to let Gmail, Chat and Meet use your content and activity in these products to personalize your experience in those apps.

When you turn this setting on, you can get experiences in Gmail, Chat and Meet that include:

  • Automatic email filtering / categorization (Primary/Social/Promotions)
  • Smart Compose (suggested text) in email
  • Smart Reply (suggested quick replies) in email
  • Summary cards above emails (travel, package tracking, and more) The setting controls app-level smart features which personalize your experience within Gmail, Chat, or Meet. Changes to this setting don’t affect your existing control over the smart features in Google Workspace setting.

When you turn off this setting, you can’t use the experiences listed above in Gmail, Chat, and Meet. To continue to use these experiences, you need to turn the setting back on.

Even if you turn off smart features in Gmail, Chat, and Meet, some smart features with individual on and off settings are still available. Some examples include:

  • Conversation summaries in Chat
  • Smart Reply in Chat Smart features in Google Workspace

When you turn on smart features in Google Workspace, you agree to let Google Workspace use your Workspace content and activity to personalize your experience across Workspace. Workspace includes apps for businesses and schools, such as Calendar, Gmail, Chat, Meet, Drive, and more. To learn more, visit the Workspace website.

When you turn this setting on, you can get experiences across Workspace that include:

  • Show events from Gmail in Calendar (such as flight itineraries and invitations).
  • Personalized search (such as keyword suggestions, file suggestions, and more relevant results) in products like Drive.
  • Ask Gemini to summarize content, create drafts, and find key information, along with other Google Workspace with Gemini features.
  • Personalized recommendations on features to use across Google Workspace services. When you turn off this setting, you can’t use the experiences listed above in Google Workspace products. To continue to use these experiences, you need to turn the setting back on.

Changes to the smart features in Google Workspace setting don’t affect your existing control over the smart features in Gmail, Chat, and Meet setting described above.

Smart features in other Google products

When you turn on smart features in other Google products, you agree to let Google use your Workspace content and activity to personalize your experience in other Google products.

When you turn this setting on, you can get experiences in other Google products that include:

  • Restaurant reservations and to-go orders in Maps (personal accounts only)
  • Suggested tickets, passes and loyalty cards in Wallet (personal accounts only)
  • Personal Intelligence in the Gemini app (personal accounts only)
  • Personal Intelligence in Search services (personal accounts only) When you turn off this setting, you can’t use the experiences listed above in other Google products. To continue to use these experiences, you need to turn the setting back on.

In addition, data sharing and personalization in other Google products may be subject to additional controls, such as Web and App Activity and DMA linked services (in the EU).

Even when the setting for smart features in other Google products is turned off, if you choose to share your Workspace data through screen actions (including screenshots), some smart features in other Google products will still be available. An example is when you use screen actions to chat with Gemini Apps.

Learn how smart features use your Google Workspace content & activity

Important: By default, smart feature settings are off if you live in:

  • The European Economic Area
  • Japan
  • Switzerland
  • The United Kingdom When smart features are on, your data may be used to improve these features. Across Google and Workspace, we’ve long shared robust privacy commitments that outline how we protect user data and prioritize privacy. Generative AI doesn’t change these commitments — it actually reaffirms their importance. Learn how Gemini in Gmail, Chat, Docs, Drive, Sheets, Slides, Meet & Vids protects your data.
