Unmark a sender as known

If you accidentally tap I know the sender, but don’t trust the contact:

  • Go to contacts.google.com.
  • At the top left, tap Main menu Other contacts.
  • Tap the contact you want to unmark as known.
  • To the right of the person’s contact info, tap More actions Delete Delete. Tip: A contact shows up as an unknown sender if you blocked them from another Google product.

Respond to event invitations

Important: If you’re an event organizer, learn how to track responses to your event.

When you get an event invitation from Google Calendar, it shows on your primary calendar. To let the event organizer know if you can make it, respond to the invitation.

Learn more about event invitations

  • When you respond to an invitation, the organizer gets a notification.
  • If you have permission to invite others to the event, you can forward an invitation email to someone else so that they can RSVP to the event. This also adds them to the event as a guest.
    • When you forward an invitation, it allows any recipient to:
      • Send a response to the organizer.
      • Invite others regardless of their RSVP status.
      • Modify your RSVP.
  • To forward an invitation, the event organizer needs to give you the “Invite others” permission or update the event’s permissions.
  • After you accept or confirm a sender, invitations appear on your calendar automatically, even if the sender is unknown. To adjust your settings, learn how to manage invitations in Calendar.

Respond to an invitation

You can respond to an event invitation from Google Calendar or Gmail.

Respond to an invitation in Google Calendar
  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • On your calendar, click the event.
  • Select a response:
    • Yes
    • No
    • Maybe
Respond to an invitation in Gmail
  • On your computer, open Gmail.
  • Open the email invitation.
  • At the top of the email, select a response:
    • Yes
    • No
    • Maybe

Propose a new time

If you can’t attend an event or need to inform the event organizer about schedule conflicts, you can propose a new time.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.

  • On your calendar, click the event.

  • Next to “Maybe,” click the down arrow Propose a new time.

  • Select a different time or day.

    • Optional: Add a message with your proposal.

  • Click Send proposal. Tip: You can’t propose a new time for:

  • Events with more than 200 guests

  • All day events

Add a note with your response

Important: Your note is visible to all invitees.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • On your calendar, click the event.
  • Next to “Maybe,” click the down arrow Add note.
  • Select your response.
  • Enter a note.
  • Click Send.

Respond to invitations forwarded through alternative email addresses

Invitations sent to an alternate email address linked to your Google Account can automatically appear on your primary Google Calendar. To update your settings for this behavior, you need to:

  • Have a Google Account.
  • Add a non-Gmail address to your Google Account.
  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • On the left, point to the name of your calendar.
  • Click Options Settings and sharing.
  • On the left, under “Settings for my calendars,” click Other notifications.
  • Check the box next to “Allow responding to invitations forwarded through alternative email addresses.”
    • If you can’t find this option, you probably don’t have an alternate email address in your Google Account. Learn about alternate email addresses.
    • When you respond to the event from your alternate email address, sign in to your Google Account.

Related resources

  • Manage invitations in Calendar
  • Create an event

Share events from your Google Calendar

To share a link to an event with other people, find responses in the calendar invite.

Learn how shared events work

Important: To prevent unwanted guests from joining your event, only share the link with the people you trust.

  • Anyone with the link to an event can:
    • Open the event details and add themselves or others as a guest. This is for public and private events.
      • Even when the “Guests can invite others” permission is off, people can still add themselves and others as guests through the shared link.
    • Find the full guest list.
    • Share the link with others.
  • If unknown guests join your event, you can:
    • Remove unknown guests from the event.
    • Duplicate the event on your calendar with the intended guests.
    • Delete the original event.

Share an event

Important: To share an event, you must sign in to Calendar with a personal Google Account.

You can share any future event from your primary calendar with others where you’re the organizer:

  • On your calendar, open an event.

  • Click Invite via link.

  • To share the link, copy it.

  • Example: You can share the link using a messaging app. Tips:

  • Guests can:

    • Open the shared link in any browser
    • Find the event details
    • Respond to the event

  • Guests signed in to their Google Account can respond with one click. Otherwise, they must fill in their email to respond.

Fix issues with event sharing

Important: To share the event, the guest permissions need to be set up correctly:

  • On your calendar, open an event.
  • At the top right, click Edit event .
  • Under “Guest permissions:”
    • Next to “Modify event,” uncheck the box.
    • Next to “See guest list,” check the box. Learn why a link can’t be created
  • On your calendar, select an event.
  • In the event window, click Edit event .
  • Check your settings. You can’t create a link if:
    • You’re not signed into a personal Google account.
    • The event is over.
    • The event isn’t on your primary calendar.
    • The event isn’t a regular event.
      • Example: A holiday, birthday, or an event not created by you.
    • You’re not the organizer. Learn why a shared link doesn’t work
  • The event may be in the past.
  • The link expired after 90 days.
  • The event is deleted by the organizer.
  • The organizer has updated guest permissions after sharing the event link. Tip: If the event was transferred, ask the new owner to generate a new link for the event.

Check the event’s settings:

  • If the event recurs: Generate a new link for every instance of the event.

Fix issues with finding shared events

Important: When you first add yourself as a guest to an event, you receive an event invitation email. You can update your RSVP in the email or on your calendar.

If you can’t find an invitation to a shared event:

  • Check your spam.
  • Check your Gmail inbox for the event invitation. If you haven’t interacted with the organizer via email before, you may get a banner “Unknown sender: Not added to Calendar yet.”
  • To add the event to your calendar, confirm that you know the sender. Learn how to respond to event invitations.

Privacy & access

Edit guest list access

Important: If the “See guest list” permission is on, you can share the link. This is the default setting.

When you turn off the “See guest list” permission:

  • The event page link won’t work.
  • Invited guests won’t see other guests on the invite.
  • Open Google Calendar.
  • Select an event.
  • In the event window, click Edit event .
  • Uncheck the box next to “See guest list.”
  • Click Save.
Fix issues when guests can’t add themselves

Important: Through the event link, you can invite up to 200 guests to an event.

If guests can access the event page but can’t invite themselves, make sure there aren’t too many guests. You can:

  • Remove extra guests.
  • Remove any groups and send the link to individual members.

Related resources

  • Create an event
  • Delete an event
  • Invite people to your Calendar event

Share events from your Google Calendar (Computer)

To share a link to an event with other people, find responses in the calendar invite.

Learn how shared events work

Important: To prevent unwanted guests from joining your event, only share the link with the people you trust.

  • Anyone with the link to an event can:
    • Open the event details and add themselves or others as a guest. This is for public and private events.
      • Even when the “Guests can invite others” permission is off, people can still add themselves and others as guests through the shared link.
    • Find the full guest list.
    • Share the link with others.
  • If unknown guests join your event, you can:
    • Remove unknown guests from the event.
    • Duplicate the event on your calendar with the intended guests.
    • Delete the original event.

Share an event

Important: To share an event, you must sign in to Calendar with a personal Google Account.

You can share any future event from your primary calendar with others where you’re the organizer:

  • On your calendar, open an event.

  • Click Invite via link.

  • To share the link, copy it.

  • Example: You can share the link using a messaging app. Tips:

  • Guests can:

    • Open the shared link in any browser
    • Find the event details
    • Respond to the event

  • Guests signed in to their Google Account can respond with one click. Otherwise, they must fill in their email to respond.

Track responses to your event

When guests respond to your invitations from Google Calendar, you:

  • Get an email notification
  • Can track their responses

Review your guest list

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • On your calendar, click the event.
  • At the top right, click Edit event .
  • On the right, under “Guests,” review the guest list.

Review a guest’s proposed time

If a guest can’t attend the scheduled event, they can propose a new time. You can review the proposal and if necessary, move the event.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • On your calendar, click an event with a proposal .
  • Under the guest list, click Review proposed time.
  • To change the proposed time, click Save.

Turn off event response notification emails

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, under “Settings for my calendars,” select the specific calendar you want to change.
  • Scroll to the “Other notifications” section.
  • Next to “Event responses,” click Menu .
  • Select None.

Manage guest response emails

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.

  • Create a new event or open an existing event and select Edit .

  • Check or uncheck the box next to “Get an email when guests respond.”

    • This option controls email notifications for a single event or a recurring event series. It does not change your global Calendar notification preferences, which are found in your main Calendar settings.

  • Click Save. When you turn off guest response emails for an event, you do not get:

  • Email notifications when guests accept, decline, or mark an event as tentative.

  • RSVP statuses from third-party users, such as Outlook.

  • Automatic email replies to the invite, such as out of office messages. Tip: Even with email notifications off, you can check when guests who use Google Calendar accept, decline, or mark an event as tentative in the guest list. However, you can’t check the RSVP status for guests who use other calendars, like Outlook.

Allow guests to forward invitations

If you give your guests permission to invite others, they can forward invitation emails to new guests. The new guests are added to the guest list and can invite others when they respond to the invitation.

If you don’t want guests to invite others:

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • On your calendar, open the event.
  • At the top right, click Edit event .
  • Under “Guests permissions,” uncheck the box next to “Invite others.”

  • Respond to event invitations
  • Create an event

Miscellaneous

Change Google Calendar settings (Miscellaneous)

To change the way Calendar looks or works, update your settings.

Change your settings

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, click General. Open Settings

Learn about the settings

Setting name

What’s it for

Language and region

Change the language, where you live, and how the date and time looks on your calendar. Learn more about language settings.

Time zone

Select the main time zone for your calendar. You can also add a second time zone. Learn how to change time zones.

World clock

Show the time from other parts of the world on your calendar. Learn how to change time zones.

Event settings

When you create a new event, you can set how long it lasts and who can join. Learn how to manage invitations in Calendar.

Notification settings

Change how you get notifications and sounds for upcoming events. Learn how to change notifications.

View options

Change the look of your calendar. Learn more about view settings.

Google Workspace smart features

Choose to personalize your experience with smart features across Workspace and other Google products. Workspace includes Google apps for businesses and school, such as Calendar, Gmail, Chat, Meet, and Drive. Learn about Google Workspace smart features.

After you turn on smart features, you can allow Gmail to automatically create events based on flight confirmations, restaurant reservations, and more. Learn about events from Gmail.

Working hours & location

If you have a work or school account with an eligible Google Workspace subscription, you can show when you’re available and where you are. This helps people know when they can meet with you. Learn how to set working hours and location.

Appointment schedules

If you have a work or school account with an eligible Google Workspace subscription, you can connect it to Stripe and require payments. Learn how to require payments for appointments.

Keyboard shortcuts

To navigate around your calendar, use your keyboard. To show a list of available shortcuts, on your keyboard, press ?. Learn more about keyboard shortcuts.

Offline

When you’re offline, you can use your main calendar. But, you can’t use other calendars or tasks. Learn how to use Calendar offline.

  • Use Dark theme in Calendar
  • Delete or unsubscribe from a calendar
  • View your day, week, or month

Get started with Google Calendar (Miscellaneous)

With Google Calendar, you can:

  • Schedule meetings and events.
  • Assign tasks.
  • Share your schedule.
  • Create multiple calendars.

Learn about supported browsers for Calendar

Important: There is no standalone desktop app for Google Calendar. Instead, use a supported web browser to open Google Calendar.

To download a supported browser, select it from the list:

  • Google Chrome
  • Firefox
  • Safari
  • Microsoft Edge Tip: For the best experience, update your browser to the latest version and turn on JavaScript and cookies.

Open Calendar in your browser

  • From a web browser, visit calendar.google.com.
  • Sign in to your Google Account.
  • To change your settings, at the top right corner, click Settings menu . Open Google Calendar

Tip: While you can’t download and install Calendar on your computer, you can use it offline. Learn how to use Calendar offline.

Related resources

  • Learn about supported browsers for Google Calendar
  • Change Google Calendar settings
  • Share your calendar
  • Create an event

Manage your events from Gmail (Miscellaneous)

If you use Gmail, emails about your events, like flights or reservations, are added to your calendar. When you open the event in Google Calendar, you can find details about the event. This includes the address, reference number, and link to the source email.

If you don’t want an event from Gmail on your calendar, you can delete it. You can also update your settings so events from Gmail aren’t added to your calendar.

Learn how events from Gmail work

  • You get events from Gmail in Google Calendar for:
    • Flights, trains, and bus reservations.
    • Hotel and restaurant reservations.
    • Events for which you get registration confirmations or tickets. This includes movies, shows, and medical events.
  • Events from Gmail stay up-to-date on your calendar. For example, if you change a reservation, the calendar event also changes. Your changes show up only on your calendar.
  • Event notifications are based on your default notification settings in Calendar.
  • By default, only you can find events from Gmail, even if you share your calendar with others. You can change the visibility of events from Gmail. Tip: If you sync Calendar with another program, you still get events from Gmail. However, some details don’t show up.

Add all events from Gmail

Important:

  • By default, events from Gmail are off if you’re in:
    • The European Economic Area
    • Japan
    • Switzerland
    • The UK
  • For all other places, events from Gmail are on. If you don’t get events from Gmail, check your Google Workspace smart features settings.
Step 1: Turn on Google Workspace smart features
  • On your computer, go to Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • Under “General,” click Google Workspace smart features Manage Workspace smart feature settings.
  • Turn on Smart features in Google Workspace.
  • Click Save. Tip: If you don’t want Gmail to add events to your calendar, turn off Smart features in Google Workspace.
Step 2: Turn on events from Gmail
  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • Under “General,” click Google Workspace smart features.
  • Check the box next to “Show events from Gmail.” Turn on events from Gmail

Delete an event from Gmail

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • Click the event from Gmail Delete event .

Hide all events from Gmail

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right, click Settings menu Settings.
  • On the left, click Google Workspace smart features.
  • Uncheck the box next to “Show events from Gmail.”
  • In the pop-up, click OK.

Learn about the limits for events from Gmail

  • Events don’t show up on your calendar if:
    • The email goes to a mail list.
    • You’re cc’d on the email.
    • The email redirects from another email account.
    • The email isn’t a confirmation for a flight, hotel, restaurant, or ticketed event.
  • Events from Gmail don’t work for:
    • Government accounts.
    • Google Accounts with data location restrictions.
    • Google Accounts that don’t have Gmail.

Report problems with events from Gmail

When Gmail finds dates and times in an email, an event may be automatically added to your calendar. If that happens and there’s a problem, like if the calendar event’s date or time is wrong, let us know.

You can send feedback about events from Gmail if:

  • The supported event type doesn’t show up on your calendar.
  • The event has wrong or missing details.
  • The event isn’t for you.
    • To find the source email of the event, open the event.
      • In the Calendar app: Select View source email.
      • In Calendar on your browser: Select View confirmation.
Step 1: Copy the email
  • On your computer, go to mail.google.com.
  • Find the email that contains the event.
  • At the top right of the email, click More Show original.
  • Click Copy to clipboard.
Step 2: Report the problem
  • On your computer, go to calendar.google.com.
  • At the top right, click Support Send feedback.
  • In the text field, describe the problem.
  • Enter the email by pasting your clipboard.
    • Windows: Ctrl + v.
    • Mac: Command + v.
  • If you want to, highlight or hide info in the screenshot.
  • Click Send.

Related resources

  • Manage the visibility of events from Gmail
  • Report problems with events from Gmail
  • Smart features & controls in Google products

View your day, week, or month (Miscellaneous)

Check your calendar to find out when you’re busy or available.

Change your calendar view

After you choose a new view, it becomes your default view until you change it.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • In the top right, choose a view: Day, Week, Month, Year, Schedule, or 4 days.

Change view settings

Choose which day your calendar week starts, set a custom view, and use an alternate calendar.

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • At the top right corner, click Settings Settings.
  • At the left, click View options.
  • Choose your settings. Changes are automatically saved. Important: The side-by-side calendars won’t work for the week or month view in Google Calendar.

Navigate your calendar

There are two ways to move between dates:

  • Use the arrows in the top left corner of the Calendar to move to recent dates in the past or future.
  • Use the small calendar in the top left corner to select a date. To return to today’s date, click Today in the top left corner.

Show shared calendars

  • On your computer, open Google Calendar.
  • On the left, choose which calendars you want to show:
    • My calendars: Calendars you own.
    • Other calendars in side-by-side day view: Primary calendars of shared people, rooms, or group calendars. Shared calendars must auto-accept invitations.

  • Create a new calendar
  • Change shared calendar settings
